Let's design a "multi-scale active LSE + coordinate" optimizer:
- Normalize sum to 1.
- Maintain global best objective and sequence.
- Phases:

  6. Upscale to 2n and repeat briefly.

Let's design algorithm with discrete continuous scaling in mind and use multi-scale from coarse to fine.

Coarse-to-fine:
- Start with small n (e.g., 128, 256) optimize to get good shape (continuous). Then upsample to larger n and continue. This can avoid local minima and load continuous structure. Previous didn't include coarse-to-fine? It used n0=8192 inits only and upscale after. Coarse may be better.
- Use n = 1024, optimize quickly; n=4096; n=8192; maybe 16384. Each stage allocate time.
- For coarse n, target objective may be higher, but shape transfers.

What about using `height_sequence_1` length 8192? We can downsample it to coarse to initialize? Maybe not necessary. Use previous as 8192 init.

Potential efficient optimizer for coarse/fine:
- Since n small to moderate, we could use scipy.optimize.minimize with L-BFGS-B on log variables? Objective non-smooth max. We can use smooth LSE with high beta. L-BFGS needs gradient, memory. But n=1024, maybe OK. However, 1 CPU and time. We can implement our own gradient with FFT.
- Use projected gradient with backtracking and momentum. We can set budgets adaptively.

def run_mirror_lse(p_init, budget, rng, start_beta=10.0, end_beta=100000.0, active_target=100):
    p = project_interior(normalize)
    logp = log(p + eps)
    step_hist list of recommended eta
    best_obj = obj
    it=0; last_improve=0; beta=start_beta
    velocity=zeros
    while time < budget:
       c = conv(p)
       M = c.max()
       obj = 2*n*M
       if obj < best: update
       # choose beta via active target
       # weights = softmax(beta(c-M)); effective = 1/sum w^2. Increase beta if effective > target, decrease if too low.
       # Or simply switch: use max weights with entropy target.
       g = 2*conv(weights,p)
       scale = max(abs(g))
       eta = eta0 / scale maybe with adaptive factor.
       # generate candidate rem = logp - eta*g; apply nullspace? Since exp normalize sum changes; no need.
       # try candidates α in [1,0.5,0.25,0.125]
       for α:
          q = softmax(logp - α*eta*g)
          obj_q = obj(q)
          if improve: p=q; break
       if no improve:
          # try reversible: maybe move +eps*(g low?)
          # add small random perturbation in log space
          noise scale decreases
          if random or stuck: logp += rng.normal(0, noise, n); p=softmax(logp)
          # Try opposite direction if gradient sign? Since we want decrease max, maybe move in + direction if logp too sparse?
          # Reverse large step: p=softmax(logp + α*eta*g)
       # momentum: maybe maintain g_smooth = 0.9g_smooth+0.1g; use that
```

def propose_candidate(budget_s=2400):
  deadline = time.monotonic() + budget_s - safety(30)
  rng = np.random.default_rng(20250607)
  global_best_a=None; global_best_obj=inf
  candidate_pool = []

  def consider(a): normalize, clean, obj=compute_obj, update global, pool

  # get previous if exists
  try: prev=np.asarray(height_sequence_1,float).flatten(); consider(prev)
  except: prev=None

  # Add initial shapes at n=8192? For coarse, we may generate at n=1024 without prev.
  n_list = [512,1024,2048,4096,8192,16384] maybe depending time.
  current = None
  for n in stages:
      if current is None:
         # generate inits at this n
         pool = [uniform, several sine, beta, random, maybe downsample prev if len> n]
      else:
         pool = [upsample(current,n), maybe upsample best_with_obj]
      # score pool, select top 3 (by obj)
      # allocate time per candidate: e.g., budget_stage * 0.5 for best, 0.25 for second, 0.25 third, but ensure min
      for each candidate: run optimizer (mirror/fista alternating) with subbudget; consider result
      # after stage, best_n maybe global best if length==n? Actually global best can be at smaller n; objective scale invariant but n affects factor; smaller n maybe higher. Keep global across all n, but for upsample use best at current or lower.
      current = global_best_a if len<=n else upsample global_best? We want increasing n: use best from previous stage (maybe not global if larger n not reached). Use `stage_best_a` selected among results at this n (or best pool). If global best from previous smaller n is better? If objective lower at smaller n, can still use its shape upsourced. ```

Let's decide final optimizer:
Function `run_smooth(p_init, budget, rng, max_n=None, use_direct_threshold=32, min_K=1, max_K=4096, warmup_iters=2000)`.
It will maintain p, logp, best.
Algorithm:
```
while time_deadline:
    c = self_conv(p); M=max; obj=2nM
    if obj < best: best=obj; best_p=p; cur_obj=obj; stuck=0; last_improve_it=it
    else stuck +=1
    it++
    # choose active weights
    if stuck < 20: K=1
    elif stuck < 80: K=min(4, ...)
    elif stuck < 200: K=8
    elif stuck < 500: K=16
    elif stuck < 1000: K=32
    elif stuck < 2000: K=64
    elif stuck < 4000: K=128
    elif stuck < 8000: K=256
    elif stuck < 16000: K=512
    else: K=min(maxK, 1024)
    # But if stuck large, maybe K=maxK
    # Add entropy: sometimes use LSE with beta if it % 8 ==0? Maybe skip for simplicity.
    w = topk_weights(c,K,power=1+min(2,stuck/1000))
    grad = 2*conv_weights_p(w,p)
    grad -= mean
    scale = max(abs(grad))
    if scale < 1e-15: break
    eta = eta_base/scale
    accepted=False
    for alpha in alphas:
       q=mirror_step(logp, grad, eta, alpha)
       oq = obj(q)
       if oq < cur_obj - 1e-15:
           p=q; logp=log(p); cur_obj=oq; accepted=True; break
       # We could skip best check because cur_obj should be best
    if not accepted:
       # Try reverse small positive? maybe if all fail, take a tiny step in negative gradient with alpha=0.01 and accept if not worse? No, can degrade.
       # reduce eta
       eta_base *= 0.7
       if eta_base < 0.01: reset eta_base=0.5
       if stuck > 200:
           # noise based on temperature
           sigma = 0.02 + 0.1*np.exp(-stuck/2000)
           logp = logp + rng.normal(0, sigma, n)
           p = softmax(logp)
           cur_obj = obj(p)
           # if cur_obj > best*1.1? keep best
           if cur_obj > best_obj * (1+0.05): p=best_p; logp=log(p); cur_obj=best_obj
           stuck = min(stuck, 100)
       if stuck > 2000 and it % 100 ==0:
           # restore best and bigger noise
           p=best_p; logp=log(p); cur_obj=best_obj
```
But this always uses topK. Is it enough? Maybe.

Let's flesh out functions.

`next_pow2`:
```
def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()
```

`fft_conv_self(a)`:
```
def fft_conv_self(a):
    n = len(a)
    size = next_pow2(2*n - 1)
    A = rfft(a, size)
    c = irfft(A * A, size)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
Use `scipy.fft.rfft`? If import `from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft`. Need ensure available. If not, fallback to np.fft? We can try import scipy.fft; but allowed. We can do:
```
try:
    from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import rfft, irfft
```
Top-level. Good.

`irfft` of complex product returns length size. For odd/even, okay. Real small negatives; clip in compute_obj.

`compute_obj(a)`:
```
def compute_obj(a):
    n=len(a); s=a.sum()
    if s < 1e-15: return inf
    a = a/s # ensure sum1
    c=fft_conv_self(a)
    return 2.0*n*c.max()
```
But if we call on non-normalized, scaling; okay. However, mutating? Use local. In optimizer, we assume sum1 and don't renormalize in compute for speed. But for safety, perhaps `compute_obj(a)` normalize (makes copy) slow. Implement `obj_from_conv(a,c)` and `compute_obj(a)` for external. In loop, p already sum1; use `c=fft_conv_self(p); obj=2*n*c.max()`.
Need compute many; avoid normalization. For initial candidates, normalize before.

`normalize_interior(a, floor=1e-15)`:
```
a = np.asarray(a,float).reshape(-1)
a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
s=a.sum()
if s<1e-15: a = np.full(len(a),1.0/len(a))
else: a = a/s
a = np.maximum(a, floor)
a /= a.sum()
return a
```
Floor 1e-15 maybe below machine relative to 1/n=1e-4? okay. Could use 1e-16. Sum adjustment after floor slight. Use floor=1e-15.

`softmax_scores(scores)`:
```
def softmax_scores(scores):
    scores = np.asarray(scores,float)
    scores = scores - np.max(scores)
    z = np.exp(scores)
    ss = z.sum()
    if ss < 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(ss):
       # fallback: take max indices equally
       n=len(scores); z=np.zeros(n); z[np.argmax(scores)]=1; ss=1
    return z/ss
```

`grad_from_dense_weights(w,p)`:
```
def grad_from_dense_weights(w, p):
    n=len(p); lw=len(w)
    size = next_pow2(lw + n - 1)
    W = rfft(w, size)
    P = rfft(p, size)
    g = irfft(W * P, size)
    return 2.0 * g[:n]
```
For c self conv, gradient with w sum1; factor 2. Good.

`grad_from_sparse(idx, w, p)`:

        k = int(k)
        l = max(0, k - n + 1)
        r = min(n - 1, k)
        if l <= r:
            # j = k - i -> as i increases l..r, j decreases k-l..k-r
            seg = p[l:r+1]??? Need align.
```

Add `wk * contrib` to g[l:r+1]. Check k=0,n=3: l=0,r=0, k-r=0,k-l+1=1 -> p[0], correct. k=1: valid i=0,1; p[1],p[0]; l=0,r=1; slice p[0:2] [p0,p1], reverse [p1,p0]; g_i: i=0 p1, i=1 p0 correct. k=4,n=3: valid i=2..4? r=min2,4=2,l=max0,4-2=2; slice p[2:3] correct. `make_topk_weights(c, k, power)`:
```
def make_topk_weights(c, k, power=1.0):
    m=len(c); k=int(k)
    if k <= 0: k=1
    if k >= m:
       vals = np.maximum(c,0.0)
       if power !=1: vals = vals**power
       s=vals.sum()
       if s<1e-300: return np.ones(m)/m
       return vals/s
    if k == 1:
       idx = int(np.argmax(c)); return (idx, [1.0])? Need dense? We can return (idx_arr,wvals) for sparse.
```
We need a weight representation to call gradient: either dense or sparse. Return tuple `(dense_or_None, idx, wvals)`. But top-level functions can't return closures; can return tuple. Simpler: function `select_weights(c,k,power)` returns `(mode, data)` where if sparse mode: `(idx, wvals)`; if dense: `w`. Then `gradient_for_weights(w_info,p)`.

Implement:
```
def select_topk_weights(c, k, power=1.0, sparse_threshold=64):
   k=min(k,len(c))
   m=len(c)
   if k <= sparse_threshold:
       if k == 1: idx=np.argmax(c); wv=np.array([1.0],float); return ('sparse', idx, wv,...)
       idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
       vals = c[idx]
       wv = np.ones_like(vals) if power==1 else np.power(np.maximum(vals,1e-300), power)
       s = wv.sum()
       if s <1e-300: wv = np.ones(k)/k else wv/=s
       return ('sparse', idx, wv)
   else:
       idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
       w = np.zeros(m)
       vals = c[idx]
       wv = ... normalized
       w[idx]=wv
       return ('dense', w, None, None)
```
But `argpartition` with k=m? if k==m special. If k close to m, `argpartition` -k invalid? k<m. If k >= m//2 maybe dense full weights `c^power` normalized and no partition could be better. Use if k > 0.5*m: dense full `c**power`. But for max K 1024 (m 32767) sparse/dense okay. For full, not used.

Power: if power large and c zeros, use maximum(vals, eps). For topK sparse, use values. For dense full, use c. Good.

LSE weights:
```
def select_lse_weights(c, beta):
    m=len(c); cm=c.max(); shift=c-cm
    # if beta*shift min < -700, underflow okay
    w = np.exp(beta*shift)
    s=w.sum()
    if not finite or s<1e-300:
       idx=np.argmax(c); w=np.zeros(m); w[idx]=1; eff=1; return w, 1
    w/=s
    ssq = np.dot(w,w)
    eff = 1.0/ssq if ssq>0 else m
    return w, eff
```
Adjust:
```
def adjust_beta(c, beta, target_eff):
    w, eff = select_lse_weights(c,beta)
    if eff > target_eff*1.2: beta = min(1e6, beta*1.15)
    elif eff < 0.5*target_eff: beta = max(1.0, beta*0.85)
    return w,beta
```
Could select LSE each iteration with current target, then adjust; if adjust, recompute? One pass okay.

if info.type == 'sparse': return grad_sparse
else: return grad_dense
```
Info can be a tuple `(kind, arr1, arr2)`. Simpler return list: `('dense', w, None, None)` or `('sparse', idx, wv, None)`. Top-level no dataclass.

Now `run_mirror_optimizer` detailed.
Parameters:
```
def run_mirror_optimizer(p_init, budget, rng, name='opt', min_eff=8, max_eff=1024, eta_start=0.35, max_eta=8.0, min_eta=0.01, topk_min=1, topk_max=1024, use_lse=True, momentum=0.0):
```
We need ensure budget positive.
Initialize:
```
p = normalize_interior(p_init)
n=len(p)
c=fft_conv_self(p); cur=2*n*c.max(); best=cur; best_p=p.copy(); logp=np.log(p+1e-300); eta=eta_start; beta=10.0; g_ema=None; start=time.monotonic(); dead = start + budget; last_iter_time? 
```
Loop while time < dead:
```
# maybe recompute p from best if current too bad after noise
c=fft_conv_self(p); M=...; cur=2nM
if cur < best - 1e-15: best=cur; best_p=p.copy(); stuck=0; cur=best? set cur=best.

   info = select_topk_weights(c,k,power=1+min(2, stuck/200))

   info = ('dense', w, None, None)
# gradient

scale = np.max(np.abs(grad))
if scale < 1e-15: break

# Try alphas: Use array [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125] but if eta maybe too small?

   delta = np.clip(delta, -8.0, 8.0)
   q = softmax_scores(logp + delta)

   oq = 2*n*c_q.max()

      if idx_alpha==0: eta = min(max_eta, eta*1.02)

      break
if not allowed:
   # try one tiny alpha 0.05 maybe with no objective? no, need eval
   # halve eta
   eta = max(min_eta, eta*0.6)
   # if long stuck, noise/restart
   if stuck > 100:
      # if current worse than best by much, restore best
      if cur > best * 1.02 or best < np.inf:
         # maybe try to restore and perturb
         if rng.random() < 0.2 + 0.0001*stuck:
             p=best_p.copy(); logp=np.log(p+1e-300); cur=best; stuck=50
      # noise
      sigma = 0.02 + 0.08*np.exp(-stuck/5000.0) + (0.001 if stuck>10000 else 0.0)
      # Apply noise in log space; but if after restore best, okay
      logp = logp + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
      p=softmax_scores(logp); cur=compute? we need eval maybe
      c=fft_conv_self(p); cur=2n*c.max()
      if cur > best*1.1:
         p=best_p.copy(); logp=np.log(p+1e-300); cur=best
      stuck = int(min(stuck, 200))
   # if stuck large, sometimes revert to best
   if stuck > 3000 and (it % 50 == 0):
      p=best_p.copy(); logp=...; cur=best; stuck=100
```

    a = normalize_interior(np.asarray(a,float).reshape(-1))

    return best_obj, best_a

`upsample_area(p,n)`:
```
def upsample_area(p,n):
    m=len(p)
    if m==n: return p.copy()
    xs=np.linspace(0,1,m,endpoint=False)
    xsnew=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
    return np.interp(xsnew, xs, p) # sum not exactly? It approximates area; normalize later.
```
For duplicating maybe more preserve? Interp okay.

Let's write each.

`obj_from_p`:
```
def obj_from_p(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.inf
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 0: return np.inf
    a = a / s
    c = fft_conv_self(a)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if m < 0.0: m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * m)
```
This normalizes each call; in optimizer we already normalized. For speed, maybe make `obj_fast(p)` without normalize. We can implement both:
```
def obj_fast(p):
   n=len(p); c=fft_conv_self(p); m=c.max(); return 2.0*n*(m if m>0 else 0)
```
Use in optimizer. `obj_from_p` calls normalize then obj_fast. Could be slight.

`normalize_interior`: Ensure copy, finite, nonnegative. If contains inf? clip. If sum<1e-300 uniform.
```
def normalize_interior(a, floor=1e-15):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = a.sum()
    n = a.size
    if n ==0: return a
    if s < 1e-300:
        a = np.full(n, 1.0/n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = a / s
    a = np.maximum(a, floor)
    s2 = a.sum()
    if s2 < 1e-300: a = np.full(n,1.0/n)
    else: a = a/s2
    return a
```

`make_initial_candidates`:
Need avoid too many convolve costs for kernel. Use vectorized lowpass via FFT maybe:
```
def lowpass_signal(h, klen):
   kernel = np.ones(klen)/klen
   h = np.convolve(h, kernel, mode='same')
   return h.copy()
```
Top-level `lowpass_signal`.

```
def make_initial_candidates(n, rng, prev=None):
    cands=[]
    if prev is not None and prev.size>0:
       cands.append(downsample_area(prev,n))  # even for upsampling
    cands.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    # Anti-random smooth amplitudes
    amplitudes = (0.25,0.45,0.65,0.85)
    for amp in amplitudes:
       h = rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
       h = lowpass_signal(h, 9)
       h = 0.5*(h - h[::-1])
       std = h.std()
       if std > 1e-12:
          h = np.tanh(amp * h / (0.5*std))? 
       # Maybe simply h = amp * h / (std+1e-12); then tanh to bound [-amp, amp]
       h = np.tanh(amp * h / max(1e-12, std))
       p = 1.0 + h
       p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
       if p.sum() > 0: cands.append(p.copy())
    # Sine random antisymmetric
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    for modes in ((1,3,5), (2,5,9), (1,2,4,8,13)):
       s = np.zeros(n)
       for m in modes:
          phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*np.pi)
          amp = rng.uniform(0.2, 0.8) / max(1.0, 0.3*m)
          s += amp * np.sin(2.0*np.pi*m*x + phase)
       p = 1.0 + 0.8*np.tanh(s)
       p = np.maximum(p,0.0)
       if p.sum()>0: cands.append(p.copy())
    # Beta shapes
    ab_pairs = ((0.6,0.6),(1.5,1.5),(0.35,2.0),(2.0,0.35),(0.8,1.5))
    xp = np.linspace(1e-6,1.0-1e-6,n)
    for aa,bb in ab_pairs:
       p = np.power(xp, aa) * np.power(1.0-xp, bb)
       if p.sum()>0: cands.append(p.copy())
    # If prev None maybe more random.
    return cands
```
Potential: `1.0 + h` with h bounded [-amp,amp] and amp<=0.85 ensures mostly nonnegative except tanh; if amp 0.85, p >=0.15. Good. This limits perturbation amplitude; might be too smooth but optimizer can increase. Maybe allow amp up to 1.2 with clip; nonnegative clips zeros. Use amp 1.0,1.2. Add.
```
amplitudes = (0.3,0.6,0.9,1.2)
h = np.tanh(h * amp / std)  # bounded [-1,1]
p = 1.0 + amp*? Actually tanh already [-1]; p=1+0.9*tanh...
```
Simplify:
```
h = np.tanh(2.0*amp*h/std)
p = 1.0 + h
```
Then h range [-1,1]. For amp >1, can get negative; clip. Good. Use amplitudes 0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,1.1.

`lower` clip removes zeros; fine.

`downsample_area(a,n)`:
```
def downsample_area(a,n):
   m=a.size
   if m==n: return a.copy()
   oldx=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m,endpoint=False)
   newx=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n,endpoint=False)
   return np.interp(newx, oldx, a)
```
For m>>n, linear interp approximate. Could area average better but okay.

`score_candidates(inits, k)`:
```
def score_candidates(cands, k):
    scored=[]
    for c in cands:
       c = normalize_interior(c)
       if c.size==0: continue
       obj=obj_fast(c)
       scored.append((obj,c))
    scored.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
    # remove duplicates? optional by sum c? no
    out=[]
    for obj,c in scored:
       if len(out)>=k: break
       out.append((obj,c))
    return out
```
No lambda.

Gradient helpers:
```
def gradient_dense(w,p):
   n=p.size
   size=next_pow2(w.size + n -1)
   W=rfft(w,size)
   P=rfft(p,size)
   g=irfft(W*P,size)
   return 2.0*g[:n]
```
Sparse:
```
def gradient_sparse(p, idx, wv):
   n=p.size
   g=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.float64)
   for ii in range(idx.size):
      k=int(idx[ii]); wk=float(wv[ii])
      if wk ==0.0: continue
      l = k - n + 1
      if l < 0: l=0
      r = k
      if r > n-1: r = n-1
      if l <= r:
         g[l:r+1] += wk * p[k-r : k-l+1][::-1]
   return 2.0*g
```
Test slope: if k-r : k-l+1, when l=0,r=k, slice 0:k+1 normal. For n large, okay. Creating reverse view with negative stride; multiplication allocates; okay. Could use `p[k-r:k-l+1]` and then `[::-1]`.

`select_topk_weights(c,k,power=1.0,sparse_threshold=32)`:
```
def select_topk_weights(c,k,power=1.0,sparse_threshold=32):
   m=c.size
   k=int(k)
   if k <=0: k=1
   if k >= m:
      vals=np.maximum(c,0.0)
      if power !=1.0: vals=np.power(vals, power, where=vals>0, out=np.zeros_like(vals))? Simpler vals=np.maximum(vals,1e-300)**power
      s=vals.sum()
      if s<1e-300: w=np.full(m,1/m)
      else: w=vals/s
      return ('dense', w, None, None)
   if k <= sparse_threshold:
      if k == 1:
         idx=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)
         wv=np.array([1.0], dtype=float)
         return ('sparse', idx, wv, None)
      idx=np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
      vals=c[idx]
      if power ==1.0: wv=np.full(k,1.0/ k)
      else:
         wv=np.power(np.maximum(vals,1e-300), power)
         s=wv.sum()
         if s<1e-300: wv=np.full(k,1.0/k)
         else: wv=wv/s
      return ('sparse', idx, wv, None)
   else:
      idx=np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
      w=np.zeros(m,dtype=float)
      vals=c[idx]
      if power ==1.0: wv=np.full(k,1.0/k)
      else: ...
      w[idx]=wv
      return ('dense', w, None, None)
```
Need `np.power` with `where` outdated. Use `np.maximum`.

`select_lse_weights(c,beta,target_eff)`:
```
def select_lse_weights(c,beta,target_eff):
   cm=float(np.max(c))
   shift=c-cm
   # if beta too large and shift very negative, exp underflow; okay
   w=np.exp(beta*shift)
   s=w.sum()
   if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
      w=np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0; eff=1.0
   else:
      w=w/s
      ssq=float(np.dot(w,w))
      if ssq > 0: eff=1.0/ssq
      else: eff=c.size
   # adjust beta
   if eff > target_eff * 1.3:
      beta2 = min(1e7, beta*1.2)
   elif eff < 0.4 * max(1.0,target_eff):
      beta2 = max(1.0, beta*0.8)
   else:
      beta2 = beta
   return ('dense', w, None, None), float(beta2), float(eff)
```
If target_eff > m, target_eff=m.

`gradient_from_info(info,p)`:
```
def gradient_from_info(info,p):
   kind=info[0]
   if kind == 'dense': return gradient_dense(info[1],p)
   else: return gradient_sparse(p, info[1], info[2])
```

`run_mirror_optimizer` detailed. Need pass `deadline`? budget. Use `time.monotonic`.

```
def run_mirror_optimizer(p_init, budget, rng, config_id=0):
    p = normalize_interior(p_init)
    n = p.size
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget))
    best_p = p.copy(); 
    p_c = fft_conv_self(p); cur = 2*n*max(p_c.max(),0)
    best_obj = cur
    logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
    beta = 10.0
    # config
    if config_id == 0:
       min_eff=16; max_eff=int(min(4096, max(128, n//16)))
       eta=0.40; momentum=0.6; use_lse=True; topk_max=int(min(4096, max(64, n//8)))
    elif config_id ==1:
       min_eff=8; max_eff=int(min(4096, max(128, n//32)))
       eta=0.80; momentum=0.0; use_lse=False; topk_max=int(min(4096, max(64, n//8)))
    else:
       min_eff=64; max_eff=int(min(8192, max(256, n//8)))
       eta=0.20; momentum=0.4; use_lse=True; topk_max=int(min(8192, max(128, n//8)))
    g_ema = None
    stuck=0; it=0
    max_eta=4.0; min_eta=0.02
    maxlog=7.0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it +=1
       # maybe recompute if p changed; cur already from last eval, but for first need
       # Use cached c? We will recompute each iter.
       c = fft_conv_self(p)
       mval = float(np.max(c))
       if mval <0: mval=0.0
       cur = 2.0*n*mval
       if cur < best_obj - 1e-15:
          best_obj = cur; best_p = p.copy(); stuck=0
       else:
          stuck += 1
       # If current is much worse than best, maybe restore
       if best_obj > 0 and cur > best_obj * 1.05 and (it > 50 or stuck > 50):
          p = best_p.copy()
          logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
          c = fft_conv_self(p)
          cur = 2*n*max(float(np.max(c)),0.0)
          stuck = 50
       # target eff
       target_eff = float(min_eff) + 0.05*stuck
       if target_eff > max_eff: target_eff = float(max_eff)
       # Decide LSE vs topk
       do_lse = use_lse
       if do_lse and (stuck < 5 or it % 4 == 0): # maybe always lse? if config use_lse true, lse mostly; topk for early stuck
           if stuck < 20:
              do_lse = False
       if do_lse:
           info, beta, eff = select_lse_weights(c, beta, target_eff)
       else:
           k = schedule_topk(stuck, topk_max)
           power = 1.0 + min(2.0, stuck/2000.0)
           info = select_topk_weights(c, k, power, 32)
       grad = gradient_from_info(info,p)
       grad -= float(np.mean(grad))
       if np.max(np.abs(grad)) < 1e-16:
           break
       if momentum > 0.0:
           if g_ema is None:
               g_ema = grad.copy()
           else:
               g_ema = momentum*g_ema + (1.0-momentum)*grad
           grad = g_ema
           grad -= float(np.mean(grad))
       scale = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
       if scale < 1e-16:
           break
       step = eta / scale
       accepted=false
       # Try alphas; if eta large, maxlog clips. Use alphas: 1,0.5,0.25,0.125
       for ai, alpha in enumerate((1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125)):
          delta = -step * alpha * grad
          # limit delta
          mask = delta > maxlog; if any? Use np.clip direct
          delta = np.clip(delta, -maxlog, maxlog)
          q = softmax_scores(logp + delta)
          cq = fft_conv_self(q)
          mq = float(np.max(cq))
          if mq <0: mq=0
          oq = 2*n*mq
          if oq < cur - 1e-14:
             p = q
             logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
             cur = oq
             accepted = True
             # eta adapt
             if ai == 0:
                eta = min(max_eta, eta * 1.03)
             else:
                eta = max(min_eta, eta * (0.9 - 0.05*ai))
             break
       if not accepted:
          eta = max(min_eta, eta * 0.7)
          if stuck > 80:
             # noise/restart
             if cur > best_obj * (1.001 + 0.02*min(1.0, stuck/1000.0)) or (stuck > 2000 and (it % 20 == 0)):
                 p = best_p.copy()
                 logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
                 c = fft_conv_self(p)
                 cur = 2*n*max(float(np.max(c)),0.0)
             sigma = 0.03 + 0.10*np.exp(-float(stuck)/3000.0)
             if stuck > 5000: sigma = max(sigma, 0.15)
             logp = logp + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
             p = softmax_scores(logp)
             cq = fft_conv_self(p)
             cur = 2*n*max(float(np.max(cq)),0.0)
             # if noise made much worse, revert
             if cur > max(best_obj*1.1, best_obj + 1e-9):
                 p = best_p.copy()
                 logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
                 cur = best_obj
             stuck = int(min(stuck, 150))
          # if very stuck, restore
          if stuck > 5000 and (it % 100 == 0):
             p = best_p.copy()
             logp=np.log(p+1e-300)
             cur=best_obj
             stuck=500
    # final ensure normalized
    best_p = normalize_interior(best_p)
    # compute exact best obj
    c = fft_conv_self(best_p)
    obj = 2*n*max(float(np.max(c)),0.0)
    return best_p, obj
```
Need be careful: `c` variable maybe not defined if before? In first loop defined. In restore after not accepted, use best_p; okay.

Let's revisit gradient formula for LSE weights using dense convolution. For objective φ=Σ w_k c_k (w normalized). c_k=Σ_i p_i p_{k-i}. ∂Σw c/∂p_t = Σ_k w_k [p_{k-t} + (if k-2t? no factor?) Wait earlier we said derivative =2Σ_k w_k p_{k-t}. Let's verify with c_0 = p_0^2. For k=0, t=0: derivative should 2p0. Formula 2 w_0 p_0 yes. For c_1 = 2 p0 p1 (if n>1). ∂/∂p0=2p1, ∂/∂p1=2p0. Formula t=0: 2w_1 p_{1}=2p1; t=1: 2w_1 p_0. Good. For c_2 = p0^2 +2p1^2? Actually if n=3, c_2 = p0^2+2p1^2+p2^2? Wait self-convolution c_2 = p0*p2 + p1*p1 + p2*p0 =2p0p2 + p1^2. I was confusion. Formula derivative wrt p1 should 2p1. 2 w_2 p_{0}? for t=1: k=2, p_{1} yes 2p1. Good. So gradient=2 conv(w,p) where conv coefficient t is Σ_k w_k p_{k-t}? Let's map: `conv(w,p)_t = Σ_k w_k p_{t-k}`? Standard convolution (w*p)_t = Σ_k w_k p_{t-k}. We need Σ_k w_k p_{k-t}. That's correlation or reversed w. Check! Important.

We defined convolution: (a*b)_m = Σ_i a_i b_{m-i}. Standard. For c_k = (p*p)_k = Σ_i p_i p_{k-i}. Derivative wrt p_t = 2 Σ_k w_k p_{k-t}. This is not standard convolution w*p at index t (which is p_{t-k}), but correlation or convolution with reversed w: (w_rev * p)_t = Σ_k w_rev_k p_{t-k_rev}? Let's derive.

Need ensure top-level helper `consider_candidate` uses `obj_fast` which assumes normalized; we normalize.
```
def consider_candidate(a, best_obj, best_a):
    if a is None: return best_obj, best_a
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0: return best_obj,best_a
    arr = normalize_interior(arr)
    obj = obj_fast(arr)
    if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_obj:
        return obj, arr.copy()
    return best_obj,best_a
```

def obj_fast(p):
   n=p.size
   s=p.sum()
   if s<1e-300: return np.inf
   # dont normalize to avoid copy? maybe if s!=1
   if abs(s-1.0)>1e-12:
      p = p/s
   c=fft_conv_self(p)
   m=float(np.max(c))
   if m<0: m=0
   return float(2.0*n*m)
```

`lowpass_signal` uses np.convolve; if `h` length small and kernel length > n? For n maybe 1024, kernel 9 okay. If n <9, we can handle:
```
def lowpass_signal(h, width):
    n=h.size
    if n <=1: return h.copy()
    width = min(int(width), n if n%2==1 else n-1)
    if width <=1: return h.copy()
    ker=np.ones(width)/width
    return np.convolve(h, ker, mode='same')
```
If even width? np.convolve with even kernel okay. Use width=9.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step heights minimizing
    2 * n * max(a * a) / (sum(a)^2).

The implementation works on the scale-invariant form with sum(a) = 1:
    minimize 2 * n * max(a * a).

Main ingredients:
- FFT-based full self-convolution and FFT/correlation-based subgradients.
- Mirror-descent / multiplicative updates with exact line searches.
- Adaptive active-set: softmax/log-sum-exp weights and top-k max weights.
- Coarse-to-fine multi-scale optimization at n = 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192, 16384.
- Several diverse initializers, including anti-palindromic smooth perturbations.
"""
import numpy as np
import time
from operator import itemgetter

try:
    from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import rfft, irfft


def next_pow2(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= m."""
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def fft_conv_self(a):
    """Full linear self-convolution a*a by FFT, length 2*n-1."""
    n = a.size
    size = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    A = rfft(a, size)
    c = irfft(A * A, size)
    return c[:2 * n - 1]


def obj_fast(p):
    """Objective for a non-negative sequence; automatically normalizes to sum 1."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    if abs(s - 1.0) > 1e-12:
        p = p / s
    c = fft_conv_self(p)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def normalize_interior(a, floor=1e-15):
    """Clip to non-negative finite values and normalize to sum 1, with a tiny floor."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    n = a.size
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 1e-300:
        a = np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = a / s
    a = np.maximum(a, floor)
    s2 = float(np.sum(a))
    if s2 < 1e-300:
        a = np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = a / s2
    return a


def softmax_scores(scores):
    """Stable softmax over score vector."""
    scores = np.asarray(scores, dtype=np.float64)
    if scores.size == 0:
        return scores.copy()
    scores = scores - float(np.max(scores))
    z = np.exp(scores)
    s = float(np.sum(z))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        z = np.zeros_like(scores, dtype=np.float64)
        z[int(np.argmax(scores))] = 1.0
        s = 1.0
    return z / s


def lowpass_signal(h, width):
    """Simple moving-average smoothing."""
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = h.size
    if n <= 1:
        return h.copy()
    width = int(min(width, max(1, n - 1)))
    if width <= 1:
        return h.copy()
    ker = np.ones(width, dtype=np.float64) / float(width)
    return np.convolve(h, ker, mode="same")


def upsample_area(a, n):
    """Resample by linear interpolation on [0,1)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = a.size
    if m == n:
        return a.copy()
    if m == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    old_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, endpoint=False)
    new_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    return np.interp(new_x, old_x, a)


def downsample_area(a, n):
    """Alias for upsample_area; also used to reduce length."""
    return upsample_area(a, n)


def make_initial_candidates(n, rng, prev=None):
    """Produce a diverse pool of initial sequences of length n."""
    cands = []

    if prev is not None:
        prev = np.asarray(prev, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if prev.size > 0:
            cands.append(upsample_area(prev, n))

    # Uniform baseline.
    cands.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    # Anti-palindromic smooth random perturbations.
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    amplitudes = (0.30, 0.55, 0.80, 1.05, 1.35)
    for amp in amplitudes:
        h = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
        h = lowpass_signal(h, 9)
        h = 0.5 * (h - h[::-1])
        sd = float(np.std(h))
        if sd > 1e-12:
            h = np.tanh(amp * h / sd)
        else:
            h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        p = 1.0 + h
        p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
        if float(np.sum(p)) > 0.0:
            cands.append(p.copy())

    # Random sine series (automatically anti-palindromic because sin(2*pi*m*(1-x)) = -sin(...)).
    for modes in ((1, 3, 5), (2, 5, 9), (1, 2, 4, 8, 13)):
        s = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for m in modes:
            phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
            amp = rng.uniform(0.15, 0.85) / max(1.0, 0.30 * float(m))
            s += amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(m) * x + phase)
        p = 1.0 + 0.9 * np.tanh(s)
        p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
        if float(np.sum(p)) > 0.0:
            cands.append(p.copy())

    # Beta-like shapes, some U-shaped or skewed.
    xp = np.linspace(1e-6, 1.0 - 1e-6, n, dtype=np.float64)
    for aa, bb in ((0.6, 0.6), (1.5, 1.5), (0.35, 2.0), (2.0, 0.35), (0.8, 1.5)):
        p = np.power(xp, aa) * np.power(1.0 - xp, bb)
        if float(np.sum(p)) > 0.0:
            cands.append(p.copy())

    # A few purely random smoothed shapes.
    for _ in range(2):
        h = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
        h = lowpass_signal(h, 15)
        p = 1.0 + 0.4 * np.tanh(h)
        p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
        if float(np.sum(p)) > 0.0:
            cands.append(p.copy())

    return cands


def score_candidates(cands, k=3):
    """Normalize and score candidates, return best k as (obj, arr)."""
    scored = []
    for c in cands:
        c = normalize_interior(c)
        if c.size == 0:
            continue
        o = obj_fast(c)
        if np.isfinite(o):
            scored.append((o, c))
    scored.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
    return scored[:max(0, int(k))]


def consider_candidate(a, best_obj, best_a):
    """Update global best if candidate is better."""
    if a is None:
        return best_obj, best_a
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return best_obj, best_a
    a = normalize_interior(a)
    o = obj_fast(a)
    if np.isfinite(o) and o < best_obj:
        return o, a.copy()
    return best_obj, best_a


def gradient_dense(w, p):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k w_k (p*p)_k wrt p.
    Returns 2 * cross-correlation(p, w), i.e.
      g_t = 2 * sum_j p_j w_{j+t}.
    """
    n = p.size
    size = next_pow2(p.size + w.size - 1)
    P = rfft(p, size)
    W = rfft(w, size)
    g = irfft(np.conj(P) * W, size)
    return 2.0 * g[:n]


def gradient_sparse(p, idx, wv):
    """Sparse version of gradient for a few selected active lags."""
    n = p.size
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    idx = np.asarray(idx, dtype=np.int64)
    wv = np.asarray(wv, dtype=np.float64)
    for ii in range(idx.size):
        k = int(idx[ii])
        wk = float(wv[ii])
        if wk == 0.0 or k < 0:
            continue
        l = k - n + 1
        if l < 0:
            l = 0
        r = k
        if r > n - 1:
            r = n - 1
        if l <= r:
            # For t = l..r, add wk * p_{k-t}.
            g[l:r + 1] += wk * p[k - r:k - l + 1][::-1]
    return 2.0 * g


def select_topk_weights(c, k, power=1.0, sparse_threshold=32):
    """
    Returns weight info:
      ('dense', w, None, None) or
      ('sparse', idx, wvals, None)
    Also normalizes weights to sum 1.
    """
    m = c.size
    k = int(max(1, min(k, m)))

    if k >= m:
        vals = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        if power != 1.0:
            vals = np.power(np.maximum(vals, 1e-300), power)
        s = float(np.sum(vals))
        if s <= 1e-300:
            w = np.full(m, 1.0 / m, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            w = vals / s
        return ("dense", w, None, None)

    if k <= sparse_threshold:
        if k == 1:
            idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=np.int64)
            wv = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
            return ("sparse", idx, wv, None)
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
        idx = idx.astype(np.int64)
        vals = c[idx]
        if power == 1.0:
            wv = np.full(k, 1.0 / k, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            wv = np.power(np.maximum(vals, 1e-300), power)
            s = float(np.sum(wv))
            if s <= 1e-300:
                wv = np.full(k, 1.0 / k, dtype=np.float64)
            else:
                wv = wv / s
        return ("sparse", idx, wv, None)

    idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
    idx = idx.astype(np.int64)
    vals = c[idx]
    if power == 1.0:
        wv = np.full(k, 1.0 / k, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        wv = np.power(np.maximum(vals, 1e-300), power)
        s = float(np.sum(wv))
        if s <= 1e-300:
            wv = np.full(k, 1.0 / k, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            wv = wv / s
    w = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    w[idx] = wv
    return ("dense", w, None, None)


def select_lse_weights(c, beta, target_eff):
    """
    Log-sum-exp weights with adaptive beta aiming for target effective sample size.
    Returns info, new_beta, effective_sample_size.
    """
    m = c.size
    target_eff = float(max(1.0, min(float(target_eff), float(m))))
    cm = float(np.max(c))
    shift = c - cm
    # beta is non-negative and shift <= 0, so no positive overflow.
    w = np.exp(beta * shift)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        eff = 1.0
    else:
        w = w / s
        ssq = float(np.dot(w, w))
        eff = 1.0 / ssq if ssq > 1e-300 else float(m)

    if eff > 1.25 * target_eff:
        beta2 = min(1e7, beta * 1.20)
    elif eff < 0.40 * max(1.0, target_eff):
        beta2 = max(1.0, beta * 0.75)
    else:
        beta2 = beta
    return ("dense", w, None, None), float(beta2), float(eff)


def gradient_from_info(info, p):
    """Convert weight info to gradient."""
    kind = info[0]
    if kind == "dense":
        return gradient_dense(info[1], p)
    return gradient_sparse(p, info[1], info[2])


def schedule_topk(stuck, max_k):
    """Adaptive number of active top lags for top-k subgradients."""
    if stuck < 8:
        k = 1
    elif stuck < 30:
        k = 2
    elif stuck < 80:
        k = 4
    elif stuck < 200:
        k = 8
    elif stuck < 500:
        k = 16
    elif stuck < 1000:
        k = 32
    elif stuck < 2000:
        k = 64
    elif stuck < 4000:
        k = 128
    elif stuck < 8000:
        k = 256
    elif stuck < 16000:
        k = 512
    elif stuck < 32000:
        k = 1024
    elif stuck < 64000:
        k = 2048
    else:
        k = 4096
    return int(max(1, min(int(max_k), k)))


def run_mirror_optimizer(p_init, budget, rng, config_id=0):
    """
    Mirror descent with adaptive active-set subgradients and exact line search.
    Returns (best_p, best_obj).
    """
    p = normalize_interior(p_init)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p, np.inf
    if budget is None or budget <= 0.0:
        return p, obj_fast(p)

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + float(budget)

    c0 = fft_conv_self(p)
    cur_obj = 2.0 * n * max(0.0, float(np.max(c0)))
    best_obj = cur_obj
    best_p = p.copy()
    logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
    beta = 10.0
    g_ema = None
    stuck = 0
    it = 0

    # Config presets.
    if config_id == 0:
        min_eff = 16
        max_eff = int(min(8192, max(256, n // 4)))
        eta = 0.40
        momentum = 0.60
        use_lse = True
        topk_max = int(min(4096, max(64, n // 8)))
    elif config_id == 1:
        min_eff = 8
        max_eff = int(min(4096, max(128, n // 8)))
        eta = 0.80
        momentum = 0.0
        use_lse = False
        topk_max = int(min(4096, max(64, n // 8)))
    elif config_id == 2:
        min_eff = 64
        max_eff = int(min(8192, max(512, n // 4)))
        eta = 0.22
        momentum = 0.45
        use_lse = True
        topk_max = int(min(8192, max(128, n // 8)))
    else:
        min_eff = 24
        max_eff = int(min(8192, max(256, n // 4)))
        eta = 0.35
        momentum = 0.50
        use_lse = True
        topk_max = int(min(4096, max(64, n // 8)))

    max_eta = 4.0
    min_eta = 0.02
    maxlog = 7.0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1

        c = fft_conv_self(p)
        cur_obj = 2.0 * n * max(0.0, float(np.max(c)))

        if cur_obj < best_obj - 1e-15:
            best_obj = cur_obj
            best_p = p.copy()
            stuck = 0
        else:
            stuck += 1

        # If we have drifted too far from the best, pull back.
        if best_obj > 0.0 and cur_obj > max(1e-12, 1.05 * best_obj):
            if it > 20 or stuck > 40:
                p = best_p.copy()
                logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
                c = fft_conv_self(p)
                cur_obj = 2.0 * n * max(0.0, float(np.max(c)))
                stuck = 40
                g_ema = None

        target_eff = float(min_eff) + 0.25 * float(stuck)
        if target_eff > float(max_eff):
            target_eff = float(max_eff)

        # Early on use sharper top-k subgradients; later use smoothed active sets.
        do_lse = bool(use_lse and stuck >= 12)
        if do_lse:
            info, beta, _eff = select_lse_weights(c, beta, target_eff)
        else:
            k = schedule_topk(stuck, topk_max)
            power = 1.0 + min(2.0, 0.5 * (stuck / 2000.0))
            info = select_topk_weights(c, k, power, sparse_threshold=32)

        grad = gradient_from_info(info, p)
        grad -= float(np.mean(grad))

        if float(np.max(np.abs(grad))) < 1e-16:
            break

        if momentum > 0.0:
            if g_ema is None:
                g_ema = grad.copy()
            else:
                g_ema = momentum * g_ema + (1.0 - momentum) * grad
            grad = g_ema
            grad -= float(np.mean(grad))

        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
        if scale < 1e-16:
            break

        step = eta / scale
        accepted = False

        for ai, alpha in ((1.0, 0.5, 0.25)[:1], (0.5,), (0.25,)):
            if ai != 0 and alpha != 1.0:
                pass
            # The tuple pattern above is only a readability artifact; simply loop:
            break

        # Direct list to avoid any confusion:
        alphas = (1.0, 0.5, 0.25)
        for ai, alpha in enumerate(alphas):
            delta = -step * alpha * grad
            delta = np.clip(delta, -maxlog, maxlog)
            q = softmax_scores(logp + delta)
            cq = fft_conv_self(q)
            oq = 2.0 * n * max(0.0, float(np.max(cq)))
            if oq < cur_obj - 1e-14:
                p = q
                logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
                cur_obj = oq
                accepted = True
                if oq < best_obj - 1e-15:
                    best_obj = oq
                    best_p = p.copy()
                stuck = 0
                if ai == 0:
                    eta = min(max_eta, eta * 1.03)
                else:
                    eta = max(min_eta, eta * (0.90 - 0.05 * float(ai)))
                break

        if not accepted:
            eta = max(min_eta, eta * 0.70)

            if stuck > 80 and ((stuck % 10) == 0 or (stuck > 250 and (it % 5) == 0)):
                # Restore best if we have drifted meaningfully.
                if best_obj > 0.0 and cur_obj > max(1e-12, 1.02 * best_obj):
                    p = best_p.copy()
                    logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
                    c = fft_conv_self(p)
                    cur_obj = 2.0 * n * max(0.0, float(np.max(c)))

                sigma = 0.03 + 0.10 * np.exp(-float(stuck) / 3000.0)
                if stuck > 5000:
                    sigma = max(sigma, 0.15)
                logp = logp + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
                p = softmax_scores(logp)
                cq = fft_conv_self(p)
                cur_obj = 2.0 * n * max(0.0, float(np.max(cq)))

                if best_obj > 0.0 and cur_obj > max(1e-12, 1.10 * best_obj):
                    p = best_p.copy()
                    logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
                    cur_obj = best_obj

                g_ema = None
                stuck = int(min(stuck, 150))

            if stuck > 4000 and (it % 100) == 0:
                p = best_p.copy()
                logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
                cur_obj = best_obj
                g_ema = None
                stuck = 400

    best_p = normalize_interior(best_p)
    final_obj = obj_fast(best_p)
    if final_obj < best_obj:
        best_obj = final_obj
    return best_p, float(best_obj)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400.0, **kwargs):
    """Search and return the best non-negative list found within budget_s."""
    seed = int(seed)
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    start = time.monotonic()
    work_budget = max(0.0, budget_s - 75.0)
    deadline = start + work_budget
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_obj = np.inf
    best_a = None

    # Use previously found global variable if present.
    prev = None
    try:
        prev = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if prev.size == 0:
            prev = None
    except Exception:
        prev = None

    if prev is not None:
        best_obj, best_a = consider_candidate(prev, best_obj, best_a)

    print("Start: obj=%.6f len=%s" % (
        float(best_obj) if np.isfinite(best_obj) else float("inf"),
        "None" if best_a is None else len(best_a)
    ))

    # Coarse-to-fine stages. Fractions sum to about 0.92; leave time for final polish.
    stage_spec = (
        (1024, 0.02),
        (2048, 0.04),
        (4096, 0.10),
        (8192, 0.24),
        (16384, 0.52),
    )

    for n_stage, frac in stage_spec:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline - 10.0:
            break
        stage_budget = work_budget * float(frac)
        if stage_budget < 35.0:
            continue
        stage_dead = min(deadline - 5.0, now + stage_budget)
        print("Stage n=%d budget=%.0fs" % (n_stage, stage_budget))

        inits = make_initial_candidates(n_stage, rng, prev=best_a)
        top = score_candidates(inits, k=3)

        if not top:
            continue

        for _o, a in top:
            best_obj, best_a = consider_candidate(a, best_obj, best_a)

        remaining = max(0.0, stage_dead - time.monotonic())
        if remaining < 20.0:
            continue

        if len(top) >= 3:
            sub_budgets = (0.55 * remaining, 0.30 * remaining, 0.15 * remaining)
            configs = (0, 1, 2)
        elif len(top) == 2:
            sub_budgets = (0.65 * remaining, 0.35 * remaining)
            configs = (0, 1)
        else:
            sub_budgets = (remaining,)
            configs = (0,)

        for i in range(len(top)):
            now2 = time.monotonic()
            if now2 >= stage_dead - 5.0:
                break
            rem_to_stage = max(0.0, stage_dead - now2)
            b = min(sub_budgets[i], max(0.0, rem_to_stage - 3.0))
            if b < 15.0:
                continue
            a_in = normalize_interior(top[i][1])
            a_out, o_out = run_mirror_optimizer(a_in, b, rng, config_id=configs[i])
            best_obj, best_a = consider_candidate(a_out, best_obj, best_a)
            print("  candidate %d obj=%.6f; global=%.6f len=%d" % (
                i, float(o_out), float(best_obj),
                len(best_a) if best_a is not None else 0
            ))

    # Final polish on the current best length.
    if best_a is not None:
        now = time.monotonic()
        rem = max(0.0, deadline - now - 20.0)
        if rem > 40.0:
            b = rem * 0.75
            a_out, o_out = run_mirror_optimizer(best_a, b, rng, config_id=0)
            best_obj, best_a = consider_candidate(a_out, best_obj, best_a)
            print("Final polish: obj=%.6f; global=%.6f len=%d" % (
                float(o_out), float(best_obj), len(best_a)
            ))

    # Fallback if something went wrong.
    if best_a is None:
        n_fb = 8192
        best_a = normalize_interior(np.ones(n_fb, dtype=np.float64))
        best_obj = obj_fast(best_a)

    final_a = normalize_interior(best_a)
    final_list = [float(x) for x in np.clip(final_a, 0.0, 1000.0)]

    print("Return list length=%d sum=%.12f internal_obj=%.9f" % (
        len(final_list), float(np.sum(final_a)), float(best_obj)
    ))

    try:
        eval_val = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
        print("evaluate_sequence(final_list) = %.9f" % float(eval_val))
    except Exception as e:
        print("evaluate_sequence check failed: %s" % str(e))

    return final_list
```
